## GTM Next — Full Data Landscape

### 1. BASE_PROD Schemas (Raw Ingestion Layer)

| Schema | Tables | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SALESFORCE** | 309 (25 base + 284 views) | Current GTM production | The existing heavily-customized Salesforce instance |
| **SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT** | 67 (all views) | GTM Next UAT | New Agentforce-based instance, UAT environment |
| **SALESFORCE_GTMNOW_UAT** | 535 (all views) | GTM Now UAT | Current SFDC mirrored to UAT (includes all custom objects) |
| **SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_LTFQA** | 0 rows currently | GTM Next LTF QA | Provisioned, no data yet |
| **CLARI** | 10 views | Current Clari | Forecasting data |
| **CLARI_GTMNEXT_UAT** | 5 views (ENTRIES, FIELDS, TIME_FRAMES, TIME_PERIODS, USERS) | Clari for GTM Next | Minimal schema so far |
| **CLARI_GTMNEXT** | 0 (just created 2026-03-25) | Clari GTM Next Prod | Empty, just provisioned today |

### 2. CERTIFIED_PROD Schemas (Transformed Layer)

| Schema | Tables | Built By |
|---|---|---|
| **GTM** | 162 tables | `eda-dbt-gtm` (252 SQL models) |
| **FINANCE** | 27 tables | `eda-dbt-em` (this project) |
| **STAGE** | 282 tables | Shared across projects |
| **COMMON** | 23 tables | `eda-dbt-common` |

### 3. GTM Next UAT vs Current SFDC — Object Gap Analysis

**Objects in both (32 shared):** `ACCOUNT`, `OPPORTUNITY`, `CONTACT`, `LEAD`, `CAMPAIGN`, `CAMPAIGNMEMBER`, `EVENT`, `TASK`, `USER`, `ASSET`, `ENTITLEMENT`, `PRICEBOOK2`, all Apttus CPQ objects (`APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__C`, `APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__C`, `APTTUS__APTS_RELATED_AGREEMENT__C`, etc.)

**35 NEW objects in GTM Next (not in current SFDC):**
- **Territory Management:** `TERRITORY2`, `TERRITORY2MODEL`, `TERRITORY2TYPE`, `USERTERRITORY2ASSOCIATION`, `OBJECTTERRITORY2ASSOCIATION`, `OBJECTTERRITORY2ASSIGNMENTRULE/ITEM`, `RULETERRITORY2ASSOCIATION`
- **Q2O (Quote-to-Order):** `QUOTE`, `QUOTELINEITEM`, `ORDER`, `Q2O_SUBSCRIPTIONFEES__C`, `Q2O_WORKERMODEL__C`, `Q2O_WORKERREPORT__C`, etc.
- **Product Catalog:** `PRODUCTCATALOG`, `PRODUCTCATEGORY`, `PRODUCTCOMPONENTGROUP`, `PRICEBOOKENTRY`, `ATTRIBUTEDEFINITION/PICKLIST`
- **Account Planning:** `ACCOUNTPLAN`, `ACCOUNTPLANOBJECTIVE`, `ACCOUNTPLANOBJECTIVEMEASURE`
- **Other:** `INDIVIDUAL`, `APPROVALWORKITEM`, `CMN_VENDOR__C`, `SLP_ACCOUNTWHITESPACE__C`, `OPM_LINKEDOPPORTUNITIES__C`

**252 objects in current SFDC NOT in GTM Next** — major customization reduction: all CX/Support objects (CASE, DEPLOYMENT, TENANT, etc.), Gainsight (JBCXM), Bizible, Consensus, custom reporting objects, many custom junction objects.

### 4. `eda-dbt-gtm` Project (252 SQL Models)

- **Source:** Reads from `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE` (current) + `BASE_PROD.CLARI` + `BASE_PROD.REDSHIFT_HISTORY` + other schemas
- **Output:** Writes to `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` (162 tables) + `CERTIFIED_PROD.STAGE`
- **Key model families:**
  - `wd_*` — "Workday Dimensional" models (account, agreement, opportunity, proposal, user, etc. + SCD2 variants)
  - `gtm_*` — GTM analytics (daily opportunity history, ACV projections, pipeline, quotas, MQL, activities, etc.)
  - `bv_gtm_*` — Dashboard views
  - `stg_*` — Staging/intermediate tables

### 5. `eda-dbt-em` Dependencies on GTM (This Project)

The EM project cross-references **7 specific GTM models** via `ref('eda_dbt_gtm', ...)`:

| GTM Model | Used In (eda-dbt-em) |
|---|---|
| `wd_account_scd2` | `stg_em_account_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics`, `arr_line_categories`, `get_arr_line_base_fn` |
| `wd_agreement_scd2` | `stg_em_agreement_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics`, + 9 finance functions |
| `wd_agreement_line_scd2` | `stg_em_agreement_line_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics`, + 5 finance functions |
| `wd_opportunity_scd2` | `stg_em_opportunity_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics`, + 4 finance functions |
| `wd_proposal_scd2` | `stg_em_proposal_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics` |
| `wd_proposal_line_scd2` | `stg_em_proposal_line_as_was`, `load_history_for_line_analytics` |
| (commented out: `wd_user_scd2`, `wd_record_type_scd2`) | Was used, now sourced locally |

**Note:** `eda_dbt_gtm` also lists `eda_dbt_em` as a dependency (circular reference for shared models).

### 6. Key Migration Risks for Finance/ARR Pipeline

1. **Schema changes in core objects** — `ACCOUNT`, `OPPORTUNITY`, `AGREEMENT`, `PROPOSAL` field-level changes will propagate through GTM SCD2 models into all ARR/ACV calculations
2. **New Territory2 model** — replaces the current territory hierarchy (`SPECIALIST_EXTENDED_TEAM__C` + custom territory objects); impacts `gtm_opportunity_team_territory_hierarchy`, `gtm_rls_*` security models
3. **Q2O replacing Apttus CPQ** — `QUOTE`/`QUOTELINEITEM` + `ORDER` objects replace parts of `APTTUS_PROPOSAL__PROPOSAL__C` / `APTTUS_PROPOSAL__PROPOSAL_LINE_ITEM__C`; this directly impacts ACV/booking calculations
4. **252 dropped custom objects** — many currently feed CX/Support models but some may feed into finance-adjacent pipelines
5. **Clari schema minimal** — only 5 tables in GTM Next vs 10 in current; forecast pipeline may need adaptation

## 74. assistant

Noted — you want naming convention suggestions for Option 1. I'll include that in the architecture when you say **"Solution"**. But here's a preview of the thinking for table naming under Option 1 (new schema for GTM Next dimensions):

### Naming Pattern Options

**Current convention:** `wd_{object}` / `wd_{object}_scd2` / `wd_{object}_v{n}` in `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM`

For the new GTM Next schema, a few approaches:

**A. Prefix-based (preserves `wd_` lineage)**
- `wd_next_account`, `wd_next_opportunity`, `wd_next_quote` (replaces proposal)
- `wd_next_quote_line` (replaces proposal_line)
- `wd_next_territory2` (replaces specialist_extended_team)
- `wd_next_account_scd2`, `wd_next_opportunity_scd2`
- Schema: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`

**B. Clean break (new namespace, no `wd_` prefix)**
- `gn_account`, `gn_opportunity`, `gn_quote`, `gn_quote_line`
- `gn_territory2`, `gn_user`, `gn_order`
- `gn_account_scd2`, `gn_opportunity_scd2`
- Schema: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`

**C. Versioned (extends existing pattern)**
- Keep `wd_` prefix but use next version: `wd_opportunity_v6`, `wd_account_v2`
- New objects: `wd_quote`, `wd_quote_line`, `wd_territory2`, `wd_order`
- Same schema: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` (coexists with current)

**D. Domain-aligned (dbt best practice for multi-source marts)**
- Schema: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`
- Tables: `dim_account`, `dim_opportunity`, `dim_quote`, `dim_territory`
- Facts: `fct_agreement_line`, `fct_quote_line`
- SCD2: `dim_account_history`, `dim_opportunity_history`

Each has trade-offs around downstream refactoring effort, coexistence clarity, and eventual migration simplicity. I'll evaluate all of these in the full architecture plan.

Key findings:
1. **eda-dbt-gtm** project: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` schema, `wd_*` tables, uses `eda_dbt_base` for `base_salesforce_*` models
2. **eda-dbt-em** project: `CERTIFIED_PROD.FINANCE` schema, depends on `eda_dbt_gtm` for `wd_*_scd2` models via cross-project refs


3. The Snowflake environment spans multiple schemas across `BASE_PROD` and `CERTIFIED_PROD`, with GTM Next UAT running in parallel to production. The critical consumption pattern flows through staging models in eda-dbt-em that reference the slowly-changing dimension tables from GTM, and the Finance pipeline depends heavily on a macro that loads historical data for analytics.

4. For the architecture, I'm going with the approved Option A using a new schema approach: creating `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT` and adding new dbt models within the existing `eda-dbt-gtm` project rather than spinning up a separate one. I'll organize this with a dedicated `models/gtm_next/` folder alongside the current GTM models, and I need to decide whether to create parallel staging wrappers in eda-dbt-em or unify them. The Sigma BI layer will need to handle the transition between the two schemas.

For naming conventions, I'm weighing three approaches: using a clean `gn_` prefix to signal the break from the current `wd_` naming, maintaining lineage with `wd_next_` versioning, or adopting domain-aligned names like `dim_account` and `fct_quote_line` that align with dbt best practices. The best path forward keeps everything in one project to preserve the DAG, configures the new schema in dbt_project.yml, creates fresh staging models for GTM Next sources, and ensures cross-project references from eda-dbt-em point to the new models.

Given the 2-year coexistence window, Sigma as a buffer layer, and the R1 scope covering Account through Agreement & Lines, I'm settling on a parallel schema architecture: keeping the existing `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE` and `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` untouched, adding `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT` for the new Fivetran feed, and creating `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT` for the managed objects that follow Option A's future-focused naming without retrofitting the old schema.

For the dbt structure, I'm staying within the existing eda-dbt-gtm project rather than spinning up something new—just adding a source for the new Salesforce schema, staging models under `models/stage_next/tables/`, and mart models under `models/gtm_next/tables/` with a dedicated schema config. I'm using a `gn_` prefix across all GTM Next tables like `gn_account`, `gn_opportunity`, `gn_quote`, `gn_contact`, `gn_lead`, `gn_event`, `gn_task`, `gn_agreement`, `gn_agreement_line`, plus territory and order objects.

The `gn_` naming gives us a clean separation from the existing `wd_` prefix since this is a different source system, keeps things short and memorable, and avoids confusion with version patterns like `v1` or `v2`—plus it sidesteps the dimensional modeling implications of `dim_` and `fct_` that don't fit our current approach. In the staging layer, I'm following the pattern of `stg_gn_{object}` pulling from base models named `base_salesforce_gtmnext_{object}`.

For consumption, Finance keeps using the existing `wd_*_scd2` models through R1, then we'll introduce `stg_em_quote_as_was` wrappers once Quote models are ready in R2, with Sigma handling the reporting stitching and eventually a GTM Next variant of the load history macro. The SCD2 strategy keeps GTM Next models starting fresh without backfilling from GTM Now, while the old models continue running until decommission—no mixing history between schemas. Each `gn_*` model gets basic uniqueness and not-null tests.

I also need to add source freshness checks on the GTM Next Fivetran tables and set up reconciliation tests to compare GTM Now versus GTM Next for overlapping entities.

Here is a clear, structured plan of action items covering the full lifecycle — from functional understanding through GAP analysis to the hybrid solution:

---

## GTM Next — Plan of Action Items

### Phase 1: Understand Functional Differences

| # | Action Item | Description | Owner | Deliverable |
|---|-------------|-------------|-------|-------------|
| 1.1 | **Attend Technical Walkthroughs** | Join BT-led technical walkthroughs per object (Account, Opportunity, Contact, Lead, Task, Event, Agreement & Lines) as outlined in the ED&A Kickoff deck | DA + AE | Meeting notes per object |
| 1.2 | **Attend BPO Syncs** | Join Business Process Owner sessions to understand business use-cases behind GTM Next data structures | DA + AE | Business requirements doc per object |
| 1.3 | **Review GTM Next Data Dictionary** | Parse the `GTM Next Program Salesforce Data Dictionary and FLS_Working-Draft.xlsx` — extract field-level metadata for all R1 objects (already done: 2,162 fields across 67 objects) | DA | Field inventory spreadsheet |
| 1.4 | **Map Workstream Ownership** | Identify which workstream (SLP, OPM, Q2O, CLM, CLC) owns which fields on each object | DA | Workstream-to-field ownership matrix |
| 1.5 | **Document Dropped Objects** | List all objects that exist in GTM Now but NOT in GTM Next (ACTIVITY_EXTENSION, HVA_TASK, MQL, OPPORTUNITY_EXTENSION, OPPORTUNITY_DETAILS, etc.) and understand business impact | DA | Dropped object register |
| 1.6 | **Document New Objects** | List all net-new objects in GTM Next (Quote, QuoteLineItem, Order, Territory2 suite, AccountPlan, Individual) and understand their business purpose | DA | New object register |

---

### Phase 2: Analyze Existing Snowflake GTM Now vs GTM Next — GAP Analysis

| # | Action Item | Description | Owner | Deliverable |
|---|-------------|-------------|-------|-------------|
| 2.1 | **Profile GTM Now schemas** | Query `BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA` for `SALESFORCE` schema — catalog all tables, row counts, column counts (already done: 307+ tables) | AE | GTM Now schema inventory |
| 2.2 | **Profile GTM Next UAT schemas** | Query `BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA` for `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT` schema — catalog all tables, row counts, column counts (already done: 37 objects compared) | AE | GTM Next schema inventory |
| 2.3 | **Field-level comparison per R1 object** | For each R1 object, compare columns between `SALESFORCE` and `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT` — identify common, dropped, new, and type-changed fields (already done: `docs/gtm_next_field_comparison.md`) | AE | Field comparison report |
| 2.4 | **Identify field name remapping** | Document where GTM Now custom fields map to GTM Next prefixed fields (e.g., `account_status__c` → `SLP_AccountStatus__c`, `acv_amount__c` → `OPM_ACVAmount__c`) | DA + AE | Field crosswalk spreadsheet |
| 2.5 | **Compliance field GAP** | Cross-reference Data Dictionary compliance tags (SOX, FIN, PII) against actual Snowflake columns — flag missing tags (already done: 318 tagged, gaps on Agreement/Order) | DA | Compliance GAP report |
| 2.6 | **wd_* model impact assessment** | For each of the 65 `wd_*` models in `eda-dbt-gtm`, assess risk level (HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW) based on source object changes (already done: `docs/gtm_next_wd_model_impact.md`) | AE | Model impact assessment |
| 2.7 | **Finance (eda-dbt-em) impact assessment** | Identify the 6 finance-critical `wd_*_scd2` models consumed by `eda-dbt-em` and assess field-level impact on ARR/ACV/TCV pipelines (already done: `docs/GTM_Next_Comprehensive_Impact_Analysis.md`) | AE | Finance impact report |
| 2.8 | **Downstream consumer inventory** | Catalog all consumers of `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` tables — Sigma dashboards, other dbt projects, ad-hoc queries — to understand blast radius | DA + BI | Consumer dependency map |

**Key GAP Analysis Queries:**

```sql
-- GAP 1: Objects in GTM Now but missing from GTM Next
SELECT t1.TABLE_NAME, t1.ROW_COUNT as gtm_now_rows, t2.ROW_COUNT as gtm_next_rows
FROM BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES t1
LEFT JOIN BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES t2
    ON t1.TABLE_NAME = t2.TABLE_NAME AND t2.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT'
WHERE t1.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE'
    AND t2.TABLE_NAME IS NULL
ORDER BY t1.TABLE_NAME;

-- GAP 2: Fields dropped per R1 object
SELECT c1.TABLE_NAME, c1.COLUMN_NAME, c1.DATA_TYPE
FROM BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c1
LEFT JOIN BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c2
    ON c1.TABLE_NAME = c2.TABLE_NAME
    AND c1.COLUMN_NAME = c2.COLUMN_NAME
    AND c2.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT'
WHERE c1.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE'
    AND c1.TABLE_NAME IN ('ACCOUNT','OPPORTUNITY','CONTACT','LEAD','TASK','EVENT',
        'APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__C','APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__C')
    AND c2.COLUMN_NAME IS NULL
ORDER BY c1.TABLE_NAME, c1.COLUMN_NAME;

-- GAP 3: New fields in GTM Next not in GTM Now
SELECT c2.TABLE_NAME, c2.COLUMN_NAME, c2.DATA_TYPE
FROM BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c2
LEFT JOIN BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c1
    ON c1.TABLE_NAME = c2.TABLE_NAME
    AND c1.COLUMN_NAME = c2.COLUMN_NAME
    AND c1.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE'
WHERE c2.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT'
    AND c2.TABLE_NAME IN ('ACCOUNT','OPPORTUNITY','CONTACT','LEAD','TASK','EVENT',
        'APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__C','APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__C')
    AND c1.COLUMN_NAME IS NULL
ORDER BY c2.TABLE_NAME, c2.COLUMN_NAME;

-- GAP 4: Data type changes between schemas
SELECT c1.TABLE_NAME, c1.COLUMN_NAME,
    c1.DATA_TYPE as gtm_now_type, c2.DATA_TYPE as gtm_next_type
FROM BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c1
JOIN BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS c2
    ON c1.TABLE_NAME = c2.TABLE_NAME
    AND c1.COLUMN_NAME = c2.COLUMN_NAME
    AND c2.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT'
WHERE c1.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE'
    AND c1.DATA_TYPE != c2.DATA_TYPE
ORDER BY c1.TABLE_NAME, c1.COLUMN_NAME;

-- GAP 5: Net-new objects only in GTM Next
SELECT t2.TABLE_NAME, t2.ROW_COUNT
FROM BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES t2
LEFT JOIN BASE_PROD.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES t1
    ON t1.TABLE_NAME = t2.TABLE_NAME AND t1.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE'
WHERE t2.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT'
    AND t1.TABLE_NAME IS NULL
ORDER BY t2.TABLE_NAME;
```

---

### Phase 3: Prepare Solution Based on GAP Analysis

| # | Action Item | Description | Owner | Deliverable |
|---|-------------|-------------|-------|-------------|
| 3.1 | **Architecture Decision Record (ADR)** | Document the approved decision: Option A (Future-Focus Hybrid) + Option 1 (New Schema). Include rationale, alternatives rejected, and constraints. | Solution Architect | ADR document |
| 3.2 | **Snowflake schema design** | Define new schemas (`CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`, `CERTIFIED_PROD.STAGE_NEXT`), DDL scripts, role grants, Fivetran verification queries | Solution Architect | Schema design doc with runnable SQL |
| 3.3 | **Naming convention specification** | Define `gn_*` prefix for all GTM Next managed objects. Full naming map: base → stage → mart → SCD2 → view | Solution Architect | Naming convention doc |
| 3.4 | **dbt project configuration** | Define folder structure (`gtm_next/`, `stage_next/`), `dbt_project.yml` changes, new vars | Solution Architect | dbt config spec |
| 3.5 | **Model specifications per R1 object** | For each R1 object: grain, source, joins, field mappings (old → new), derived columns, dedup logic, source_system discriminator | Solution Architect | Model spec per object |
| 3.6 | **SCD2 strategy** | Fresh history chains from GTM Next (no backfill from GTM Now). Define which objects get SCD2 (Account, Opportunity, Agreement, Agreement Line) | Solution Architect | SCD2 strategy doc |
| 3.7 | **Testing strategy** | PK integrity, source freshness, referential integrity, row count validation, compliance field checks | Solution Architect | Test plan |
| 3.8 | **eda-dbt-em consumption strategy** | R1: zero changes to Finance. R2+: new `gn_scd2_wrappers/` folder for Quote/QuoteLineItem migration | Solution Architect | Finance migration plan |
| 3.9 | **Sigma BI stitching patterns** | Union pattern, preference pattern, side-by-side validation pattern — all with runnable SQL | Solution Architect | BI stitching playbook |

### Phase 4: Plan & Approach for Future Hybrid Solution

| # | Action Item | Description | Owner | Deliverable |
|---|-------------|-------------|-------|-------------|
| 4.1 | **Define release-by-release roadmap** | Map R1 → R4 scope: which objects go live in each release, which `wd_*` models get `gn_*` equivalents, which get deprecated | Program + SA | Release roadmap |

## 141. assistant

Great research. Now I have a clear picture of the current patterns and industry best practices. Let me present the options.

## Naming Convention Options

Here are the key constraints to consider:

1. **dbt requires globally unique model names** within a project — you cannot have two `wd_account.sql` files
2. **65+ existing `wd_*` models** with cross-project `ref('eda_dbt_gtm', 'wd_*')` calls from `eda-dbt-em`
3. **2-year coexistence** — both naming systems run in parallel
4. **FY28 end state** — GTM Now deprecated, GTM Next becomes the single source
5. Current `source_system = 'SFDC'` discriminator already exists in all `wd_*` models

### Option 1: `gn_*` (Simple Source Prefix)

```
wd_account          → gn_account
wd_opportunity_scd2 → gn_opportunity_scd2
stg_wd_opportunity  → stg_gn_opportunity
```

- **Pro:** Short, clean, mirrors existing `wd_*` pattern. Easy to grep, filter, tag.
- **Con:** `gn_` has no inherent meaning to newcomers. At FY28 cutover, do you rename `gn_*` to `wd_*`? Or live with `gn_*` forever? Creates a "third naming era" problem.

### Option 2: `wd_gn_*` (Combined Prefix)

```
wd_account          → wd_gn_account
wd_opportunity_scd2 → wd_gn_opportunity_scd2
stg_wd_opportunity  → stg_wd_gn_opportunity
```

- **Pro:** Preserves `wd_` brand identity — signals "this is a curated wide dimension." The `gn` qualifier says "from GTM Next."
- **Con:** Verbose. Mixes layer (`wd_`) with source (`gn`). At cutover, do you rename back to `wd_*`? Creates awkward patterns like `wd_gn_account_scd2`.

### Option 3: `wd_*` Reuse with Schema-Only Separation (dbt alias trick)

```
File: gn_account.sql
Config: alias='wd_account', schema='gtm_next'
Physical: CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account
```

- **Pro:** Consumers see the same `wd_*` naming in both schemas. At cutover, just swap the schema pointer. Most future-proof for eventual convergence.
- **Con:** dbt model names in `ref()` must still differ (file is `gn_account.sql` but alias is `wd_account`). Cross-project refs become confusing: `ref('eda_dbt_gtm', 'gn_account')` returns a table named `wd_account`. Developers will struggle with the indirection.

### Option 4: `wd_*` + Source Discriminator Suffix (dbt-aligned)

```
wd_account              → wd_account_gtmnext
wd_opportunity_scd2     → wd_opportunity_gtmnext_scd2
stg_wd_opportunity      → stg_wd_opportunity_gtmnext
```

- **Pro:** Single `wd_` namespace. Clear which instance. Aligned with dbt's "encode the source in the name" guidance.
- **Con:** Very long names, especially with existing suffixes (`wd_specialist_extended_team_gtmnext_daily_snapshot`). Suffix position conflicts with `_scd2` / `_v5` suffixes. At cutover, need to rename or drop the `_gtmnext` suffix.

### Option 5: `wdn_*` (Evolution of `wd_*`)

```
wd_account          → wdn_account
wd_opportunity_scd2 → wdn_opportunity_scd2
stg_wd_opportunity  → stg_wdn_opportunity
```

- **Pro:** Clearly an evolution of `wd_*` (wd-next). Short. Intuitive once explained. Same character length as `wd_`. At cutover, `wdn_*` simply becomes the canonical name — no rename needed.
- **Con:** Subtle visual difference from `wd_*` — could cause typos/confusion in code reviews.

---

## My Recommendation

Before I formalize the plan, I want to understand your preference on one key question:

Now I have a good understanding of:

1. Current naming patterns in eda-dbt-gtm: `wd_*` prefix for curated/wide tables, `stg_wd_*` for staging, `bv_*` for views, `ref_*` for lookups, `gtm_*` for GTM marts
2. dbt best practices: layer prefix + source discriminator (`stg_<source>__<entity>`), separate sources per org instance
3. The `wd_` prefix is an organizational choice, not dbt standard

Now let me think about the naming options:


I'm weighing three main approaches: using a new `gn_` prefix that's short and distinct but breaks from dbt conventions, combining it as `wd_gn_*` to show lineage but risking verbosity, or keeping `wd_*` names and relying on schema separation instead.

The schema-only approach won't work since dbt requires unique model names across the project and `ref()` would become ambiguous. Option D encodes the source directly in the name like `wd_gtmnext_account`, which is clearer about lineage but creates longer names. Option E uses the dbt-standard double-underscore pattern to separate the source discriminator.

Given that the existing `wd_*` prefix is already deeply embedded across 65+ models with cross-project dependencies, and these need to coexist for two years before the old GTM Now models deprecate, I'm leaning toward either keeping `wd_*` with a `_gtmnext` suffix like `wd_account_gtmnext`, or using the double-underscore convention at the staging level while maintaining the `wd_` pattern for curated models in the new schema. The real tension here is whether `gn_*` is a permanent naming convention or a temporary bridge to eventual convergence—if GTM Next models are meant to replace the canonical `wd_*` tables by FY28, then naming them differently now creates a future rename burden that we should avoid.

## 142. assistant

Excellent choice. Option 3 with rename-to-`wd_*` at cutover is the most architecturally clean end state. Let me work out the full implementation details.

2. **Rename back to `wd_*`** at FY28 cutover — so the end state is that the GTM Next models become the canonical `wd_*` models

**The approach:**

- At cutover, rename the file from `gn_account.sql` to `wd_account.sql`, move it from `gtm_next/` to `gtm/`, and point `ref()` calls to it

The key constraint here is that both schemas need physical `wd_*` table names, but dbt model names must be distinct across the project, so I'll use the internal naming convention for the GTM Next models while aliasing them to the canonical names in Snowflake. So the question is: what do we call the dbt model files internally?

Since the user selected Option 3 (alias trick), the approach would be:
- **File name**: Some internal name that's different from `wd_*` to avoid dbt name collision
- **Alias**: `wd_<object>` → physical table name in Snowflake
- **Schema**: `gtm_next` → lands in `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`

So the physical table would be:
- GTM Now: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM.wd_account`
- GTM Next: `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account`

Same table name, different schema. This is elegant for the end state.

For the dbt model file names, I'm weighing three naming conventions: `gn_account`, `wd_account_next`, or `gtmnext_account`. The `gn_` prefix seems cleanest since it clearly signals "GTM Next" internally while the alias handles the public-facing name. At cutover, I'd delete the old GTM Now model, rename the GTM Next file to match the standard naming, update the schema, drop the alias, and then do a find-and-replace across all refs to point to the new model name. This keeps the internal naming temporary and the physical table names consistent throughout. The user is choosing Option 3, so the physical Snowflake table should stay named `wd_*`, but the dbt model name can differ from the table name—that's the whole point of the alias trick. I should clarify the plan: use `gn_*` for the dbt model files and `ref()` calls during coexistence, then at cutover do a simple find-and-replace to rename everything back to `wd_*`.

## 156. assistant

That's actually the cleanest architectural approach — same `wd_*` names, different schema. There are two ways to achieve this:

---

### Approach A: Same dbt Project + Alias

The file name must be different (dbt requires unique model names within a project), but the **physical Snowflake table name** is identical via `alias`:

```
dbt File Name              Snowflake Physical Table
─────────────────────      ───────────────────────────────────
wd_account.sql         →   CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM.wd_account
wd_gn_account.sql      →   CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account   (alias: wd_account)
```

```yaml
# wd_gn_account.yaml (GTM Next version)
config:
  alias: wd_account          # <-- physical table name stays wd_account
  schema: gtm_next            # <-- just a different schema
```

- **Snowflake consumers** see identical names across schemas — seamless
- **dbt developers** use `ref('wd_gn_account')` internally — slight difference
- **At FY28 cutover**: rename file `wd_gn_account.sql` → `wd_account.sql`, drop old, update refs

---

### Approach B: Separate dbt Project (`eda-dbt-gtm-next`)

Create a **new dbt project** entirely. Now you CAN use the exact same `wd_*` file names:

```
eda-dbt-gtm/models/gtm/tables/wd_account.sql        →  CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM.wd_account
eda-dbt-gtm-next/models/gtm/tables/wd_account.sql   →  CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account
```

```sql
-- Cross-project ref distinguishes by project name, not model name
FROM {{ ref('eda_dbt_gtm', 'wd_account') }}          -- GTM Now
FROM {{ ref('eda_dbt_gtm_next', 'wd_account') }}     -- GTM Next
```

- **Snowflake consumers** see identical `wd_*` names in both schemas
- **dbt developers** use the SAME `wd_account` name — just different project qualifier
- **At FY28 cutover**: point `eda-dbt-em` refs from `eda_dbt_gtm` → `eda_dbt_gtm_next`, then retire old project

---

### Comparison

| Dimension | Approach A (Same Project + Alias) | Approach B (Separate Project) |
|-----------|-----------------------------------|-------------------------------|
| **Snowflake table names** | `wd_*` in both schemas | `wd_*` in both schemas |
| **dbt model file names** | Must differ (`wd_gn_*` vs `wd_*`) | Identical (`wd_*` in both) |
| **ref() in dbt** | `ref('wd_gn_account')` | `ref('eda_dbt_gtm_next', 'wd_account')` |
| **FY28 cutover effort** | Rename files + update all refs | Change project name in refs only |
| **Operational overhead** | One project to maintain | Two projects (CI/CD, deps, configs) |
| **Macro/test sharing** | Shared automatically | Need `dependencies.yml` or packages |
| **DAG visibility** | Single DAG, easy to see both | Split DAG across projects |
| **Best for** | Smaller team, simpler ops | Larger team, clean separation |

The user is asking about using the exact same `wd_*` model names but in a different Snowflake schema. This is essentially Option 3 that they already selected — but they want to understand the mechanics and feasibility more deeply.

The key challenge is: **dbt requires globally unique model names within a project**. You cannot have two files named `wd_account.sql` in the same dbt project.

But there are ways around this:

1. **Different file name + alias** — File is `wd_account_next.sql` but `alias: wd_account` in `GTM_NEXT` schema. This is what we discussed.


2. **Separate dbt projects** — If GTM Next lives in its own project like `eda-dbt-gtm-next`, you can reuse the same model names since each project is independent. The tables would live in different schemas (`GTM` vs `GTM_NEXT`), and you'd reference across projects using the three-argument `ref()` syntax.
3. **Same project, different folders** —

Now I'm weighing the trade-offs between using an alias to keep the same model name in one project versus splitting into separate projects entirely. With the alias approach, the file name changes but the Snowflake table stays the same, though refs need updating at cutover. The separate project approach lets you keep identical file and model names across projects, with cross-project refs handling the distinction. ', 'wd_account')` vs `ref('eda_dbt_gtm', 'wd_account')`
- At cutover: just change the project name in cross-project refs
- Pro: EXACT same model names! No renaming needed at cutover for the models themselves.
- Con: Need to create and maintain a separate dbt project. More operational overhead. But architecturally very clean.

**Option C: Same project with generate_model_name macro**
- Use a custom `generate_model_name` macro that strips a folder-based prefix
- Both files could conceptually be "wd_account" but live in different folders
- This is hacky and not recommended

Actually, Option B (separate dbt project) is the cleanest way to achieve exactly what the user wants: same `wd_*` names, different schemas. And it's architecturally sound — many organizations use separate dbt projects for different source systems.

Though there's another angle worth considering. Within the same project, I could use dbt's `alias` config to make the physical table names match across schemas while keeping the internal file names different. That way consumers see `wd_account` in both `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` and `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT`, but the `ref()` calls inside dbt would still point to different files. So it really comes down to whether the developer experience of different `ref()` names matters more than the operational simplicity of a single project.

The separate project approach is actually quite elegant — the user might be leaning that way.

I'd recommend **Approach A: Same project + alias** with `wd_gn_` as the internal dbt prefix. Here's why:

### My Recommendation: `wd_gn_*` (internal) + `wd_*` (Snowflake physical)

**Why this is the best architecture decision:**

1. **Snowflake consumers see zero change** — Both schemas have `wd_account`, `wd_opportunity_scd2`, etc. Sigma BI, ad-hoc queries, Finance — they all query `wd_*` tables regardless of which schema. The stitching pattern becomes a simple schema swap:

```sql
-- Sigma just switches schema, same table names
SELECT * FROM CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM.wd_account          -- GTM Now
SELECT * FROM CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account     -- GTM Next (same name!)
```

2. **dbt developers have clear lineage** — `wd_gn_` is self-documenting. You see `wd_gn_account` and instantly know: "it's a `wd_account` equivalent, from GTM Next." No ambiguity, no learning curve.

3. **Single project, single DAG** — No operational overhead of a second project. Shared macros (`audit_columns()`, `dbt_tags`), shared CI/CD, one `dbt build` command.

4. **FY28 cutover is a clean rename** — Just strip `_gn` from file names and update refs. The Snowflake tables don't change at all because aliases already match.

5. **`wd_gn_` beats `wd_gnx_`** — Simpler, shorter, no need for the "x." The "gn" already carries the meaning (GTM Next). Every extra character is friction multiplied across 30+ models and hundreds of refs.

### How It Looks End-to-End

```
Layer                  GTM Now                         GTM Next
─────────────────      ─────────────────────           ──────────────────────────
Snowflake table        GTM.wd_account                  GTM_NEXT.wd_account
dbt file name          wd_account.sql                  wd_gn_account.sql
dbt ref()              ref('wd_account')               ref('wd_gn_account')
YAML alias             alias: wd_account               alias: wd_account
YAML schema            schema: gtm                     schema: gtm_next
source_system col      'SFDC'                          'SFDC_GTMNEXT'
```

### FY28 Cutover (Minimal Effort)

```
Step 1: Delete old wd_account.sql (GTM Now)
Step 2: Rename wd_gn_account.sql → wd_account.sql
Step 3: Remove alias config (file name = table name now)
Step 4: Change schema: gtm_next → schema: gtm
Step 5: Global find-replace ref('wd_gn_') → ref('wd_') across eda-dbt-em
```

Snowflake tables never move, never rename — just the dbt internals get cleaned up.

---

Want me to finalize this as the plan and update the architecture document with `wd_gn_*` as the naming standard?

### Options for GTM Next Schema

**Mirror the existing pattern (GTM + qualifier):**

| Schema Name | Stage Schema | Feel |
|-------------|--------------|------|
| `GTM_NEXT` | `STAGE_NEXT` | Obvious, descriptive. Everyone knows what it is instantly. But "NEXT" implies temporary — what happens at FY28 when it's no longer "next"? |
| `GTM_V2` | `STAGE_V2` | Versioned. Clear evolution. But same problem — after cutover, "v2" is meaningless. |
| `GTM_GN` | `STAGE_GN` | Matches the `wd_gn_` dbt prefix. Short. But `GN` alone is cryptic in Snowflake to someone who doesn't know the project. |

**Future-proof (designed for the end state):**

| Schema Name | Stage Schema | Feel |
|-------------|--------------|------|
| `GTM` (reuse) | `STAGE` (reuse) | At FY28 cutover, the new tables ARE the GTM tables. No schema rename needed. But during coexistence you'd need to rename the old one first (e.g., `GTM` → `GTM_LEGACY`). Risky — breaks all existing consumers. |
| `GTM_AF` | `STAGE_AF` | Agentforce. Tied to the platform. But Agentforce is a Salesforce brand — could change. |

**Clean coexistence + easy cutover:**

| Schema Name | Stage Schema | Feel |
|-------------|--------------|------|
| `GTM_NEXT` → rename to `GTM` at FY28 | `STAGE_NEXT` → rename to `STAGE` | Start as `GTM_NEXT`, then at cutover: rename old `GTM` → `GTM_ARCHIVE`, rename `GTM_NEXT` → `GTM`. All consumers point to `GTM` with zero ref changes. |
| `GTMNEXT` | `STAGENEXT` | No underscore. Reads as one word. Mirrors `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT` raw schema from Fivetran. Consistent with upstream naming. |

### My Recommendation: `GTM_NEXT` with FY28 rename strategy

**Why:**

1. **Immediately obvious** — anyone querying `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT.wd_account` knows exactly what they're looking at
2. **Mirrors Fivetran upstream** — raw data lands in `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT`, certified data lives in `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT` — the naming flows naturally through the pipeline
3. **Clean FY28 cutover path:**
   - `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` → rename to `GTM_ARCHIVE` (or `GTM_LEGACY`)
   - `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM_NEXT` → rename to `GTM`
   - Result: all consumers querying `GTM` now get GTM Next data. Zero query changes needed.
4. **"NEXT" is temporary by design** — it lasts exactly as long as the coexistence period, then disappears via rename

The alternative `GTMNEXT` (no underscore) also works well if you want consistency with the Fivetran `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT` schema naming.

**Date:** 2026-03-26

## Action Item 3: Field Crosswalk & Mapping

### Objective

Build a comprehensive, field-level crosswalk document mapping every GTM Now column to its GTM Next equivalent for each R1 object. Identify and document all derived columns that require new derivation logic.

#### 3.1 Crosswalk Structure (per R1 Object)

| Column | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| `gtm_now_api_name` | Current Salesforce API name |
| `gtm_now_alias` | Column alias in `wd_*` model |
| `gtm_next_api_name` | New Salesforce API name |
| `gtm_next_alias` | Proposed column alias in `wd_gn_*` model |
| `workstream` | Owning workstream (SLP, OPM, Q2O, CLM, CLC, CMN) |
| `mapping_type` | DIRECT (1:1 rename), DERIVED (logic change), DROPPED, NEW |
| `derivation_logic_now` | How the value is currently derived in `wd_*` SQL |
| `derivation_logic_next` | How it should be derived in `wd_gn_*` SQL |
| `compliance` | SOX / FIN / PII tag from Data Dictionary |
| `question_or_clarification` | Open question for SFDC team |

## Action Item 5: Solution Design & Architecture

### Objective

Design the complete Snowflake and dbt architecture for ingesting, transforming, and serving GTM Next data alongside GTM Now.

#### 5.1 Snowflake Schema Architecture

**Data Mesh Context:** Existing `CERTIFIED_PROD.GTM` tables are being distributed into domain-aligned databases (IA-DB Restructure): `FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV]`, `SALES_[ENV]`, `MARKETING_[ENV]`, `FINANCE_[ENV]`.

**GTM Next Decision:** Consolidate all GTM Next managed objects into a **single database** `FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_[ENV]` with `MANAGED` and `STAGING` schemas during the coexistence period. Domain-split deferred to FY28 cutover.

```
BASE_PROD (Raw)
  |-- SALESFORCE (GTM Now, 307+ tables)
  |-- SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT (GTM Next)
        |
        v
Domain DBs (GTM Now, unchanged)          FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_[ENV] (NEW)
  FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV].MANAGED         .MANAGED
    wd_account, wd_record_type, ...           wd_account (alias of wd_gn_account)
  SALES_[ENV].MANAGED                         wd_opportunity (alias of wd_gn_opp)
    wd_opportunity_v5, wd_lead, ...           wd_contact, wd_lead, wd_task,
  MARKETING_[ENV].MANAGED                     wd_event, wd_agreement,
    wd_campaign_*, wd_mql_*, ...              wd_quote, wd_quote_line,
  FINANCE_[ENV].MANAGED                       wd_territory2, wd_*_scd2
    (em models)                             .STAGING
                                              stg_wd_gn_*
        |                                       |
        +-----------+---------------------------+
                    v
        FINANCE_[ENV].MANAGED (eda-dbt-em, unchanged R1)
                    v
              Sigma BI (Stitching Layer)
```

**Key architectural decision:** Physical table names in `FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_[ENV].MANAGED` use the `wd_*` prefix (via dbt alias), keeping consumer-facing names familiar. Internal dbt model files use `wd_gn_*` prefix for developer disambiguation.

**Snowflake DDL:** Requires DBA execution -- create `FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_DEV` and `FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_PROD` databases with `MANAGED` + `STAGING` schemas, grants to `ANALYST_ROLE` and dbt service roles. DDL maintained separately in engineering runbook.

#### 5.2 Naming Conventions

**Dual-naming strategy:** dbt model files use `wd_gn_*` for developer clarity. Snowflake physical tables use `wd_*` (via dbt alias) for consumer familiarity. Discriminators: `source_system = 'SFDC_GTMNEXT'` and database `FOUNDATIONAL_NEXT_[ENV].MANAGED`.

| Layer | GTM Now | GTM Next (dbt internal) | GTM Next (Snowflake physical) |

```
dbt Project: eda-dbt-common
Target: FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV].MANAGED
Source System Tag: source_system = 'SFDC_GTMNEXT'
```

---

**Note 2 — Add as a sticky note group titled "NAMING CONVENTIONS":**

```
NAMING CONVENTIONS

• Standard R1 objects → wd_<object> (e.g., wd_account, wd_opportunity)
• Shared objects in same DB/schema → wd_<object>_gnx (e.g., wd_user_gnx, wd_record_type_gnx)
• SCD2 history → wd_<object>_scd2 or wd_<object>_gnx_scd2
• Shared date dimension → wd_date (no suffix — not Salesforce dependent)
• Base models → base_salesforce_gtmnext_<object>
• dbt internal files → wd_gn_<object>.sql (alias to wd_<object> in Snowflake)
• source_system column = 'SFDC_GTMNEXT' on every output table
```

---

**Note 3 — Add as a sticky note group titled "5-LAYER DATA FLOW":**

```
5-LAYER DATA FLOW

Layer 1 — SOURCE: Salesforce GTM Next (Agentforce)
Layer 2 — RAW: RAW_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT (Fivetran CDC, immutable)
Layer 3 — BASE: BASE_PROD_NEXT (1:1 views on raw, cast/rename, soft-delete filter)
Layer 4 — TRANSFORM: eda-dbt-common (joins, enrichment, aliasing, business logic)
Layer 5 — MANAGED: FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV].MANAGED (wd_* tables + wd_*_scd2)

Max 4 hops: Source → Base → (Intermediate) → Managed
```

---

**Note 4 — Add as a sticky note group titled "DESIGN PRINCIPLES":**

```
DESIGN PRINCIPLES

1. Grain integrity — every table documents grain in SQL + YAML, PK tested (unique + not_null)
2. No business logic in views — push logic into wd_* managed tables
3. CTE-first SQL — with source as (...), enriched as (...), final as (...)
4. Fan-in at intermediate, fan-out at managed — wd_* models never reference other wd_*
5. ref() / source() only — never hardcode table names
6. Audit columns — _loaded_at, _updated_at via audit_columns() macro on every table
7. Currency variant — all monetary values specify USD_CURRENT, USD_HIST, or USD_ACTUAL
8. No hardcoded fiscal periods — use get_fiscal_quarter / get_fiscal_attributes macros
```

---

**Note 5 — Add as a sticky note group titled "COEXISTENCE RULES":**

```
COEXISTENCE RULES

1. GTM Now pipeline never stops — existing wd_* in domain DBs continue until explicit decommission
2. No cross-references — GTM Next models never ref() GTM Now models and vice versa
3. Independent SCD2 chains — GTM Next scd2 starts fresh from go-live, no backfill from GTM Now
4. source_system discriminator — 'SFDC_GTMNEXT' vs 'SFDC'
5. Database discriminator — FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV] vs domain DBs (SALES, MARKETING, FINANCE)
6. Independent CI/CD — dbt build --select tag:gtm_next (no impact on GTM Now)
7. Zero changes to eda-dbt-em for R1 — Finance adopts GTM Next SCD2 tables in R2+
```

---

**Note 6 — Add as a sticky note group titled "SHARED OBJECTS (_gnx)":**

```
SHARED OBJECTS — _gnx SUFFIX

These 5 objects exist in FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV].MANAGED for BOTH GTM Now and GTM Next:

GTM Now          → GTM Next
wd_user          → wd_user_gnx
wd_user_scd2     → wd_user_gnx_scd2
wd_user_role     → wd_user_role_gnx
wd_record_type   → wd_record_type_gnx
gtm_user_manager_hierarchy → gtm_user_manager_hierarchy_gnx

wd_date — SHARED (no rename, no Salesforce dependency)
```

---

**Note 7 — Add as a sticky note group titled "NO NAMING CONFLICT":**

```
NO NAMING CONFLICT — IA-DB RESTRUCTURE

GTM Now wd_* objects are distributed to domain-specific DBs:
• Account, Opportunity → SALES_[ENV].MANAGED
• Contact, Lead, Task, Event → MARKETING_[ENV].MANAGED
• Agreement, Agreement Line → FINANCE_[ENV].MANAGED

GTM Next wd_* objects land in:
• FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_[ENV].MANAGED

Different databases = zero naming collision for standard objects.
Only shared foundational objects (User, RecordType, UserRole) use _gnx suffix.
```

---

**Note 8 — Add as a sticky note group titled "ENVIRONMENT MATRIX":**

```
ENVIRONMENT MATRIX

PROD: RAW_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT → BASE_PROD_NEXT → FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_PROD.MANAGED
QA:   RAW_QA.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT   → BASE_QA_NEXT   → FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_QA.MANAGED
DEV:  RAW_DEV.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT  → BASE_DEV_NEXT  → FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_DEV.MANAGED
```

---

**Note 9 — Add as a sticky note group titled "DOWNSTREAM CONSUMERS":**

```
DOWNSTREAM CONSUMERS

1. Sigma BI — reads both GTM Now domain DBs + GTM Next FOUNDATIONAL_ASSETS_PROD.MANAGED
   Stitching via source_system = 'SFDC' vs 'SFDC_GTMNEXT'

2. eda-dbt-em (Finance) — consumes SCD2 tables via cross-project ref()

3. Ad-hoc queries — both pipelines queryable independently
```

Here's how to get your Miro API token:

**Step 1 — Go to Miro Developer Settings**

Open this URL in your browser:
```
https://miro.com/app/settings/user-profile/apps
```

**Step 2 — Create a New App**

1. Click **"Create a new app"**
2. Give it a name: `GTM Next Board Builder`
3. Select your team (the one that owns the board)
4. Click **"Create app"**

**Step 3 — Set Permissions (Scopes)**

In the app settings, under **Permissions**, enable these scopes:
- `boards:read`
- `boards:write`

Click **"Install app and get OAuth token"** (or **"Save"** first, then install).

**Step 4 — Copy the Access Token**

After installing, Miro will show you an **Access Token** (starts with something like `eyJ...`). Copy this token.

**Step 5 — Verify it works**

You can test it by running this in your terminal:
```bash
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer YOUR_TOKEN_HERE" https://api.miro.com/v2/boards/uXjVGqWc4qE=
```

If it returns your board details (name: "GTM Next- Solution Approach"), the token is working.

## Task / Event (Activity Objects)

### Field Analysis Summary

| Metric | Task | Event |
|---|---|---|
| **Common fields** (both GTM Now & Next) | 136 | 128 |
| **Dropped fields** (removed in GTM Next) | ~30 | ~33 |
| **New fields** (only in GTM Next) | ~12 | ~17 |

### Key Dropped Integrations (Task & Event)
- **Gong** -- `GONG__ACTIVITY_SOURCE__C`, `GONG__ASSOCIATED_ACCOUNTS__C`, `GONG__FLOW_*` (all dropped)
- **Bizible** -- `BIZIBLE2__*` (all dropped)
- **HVA Task** -- `HVA_TASK_ID__C`, `HVA_TASK_URL__C`, `HVA_TASK_PRODUCTS__C` (dropped)
- **Regie AI** -- `REGIEAI__*` (dropped from Task, but new on Contact)

### Key New Fields (Task & Event)
- **SumoLogic/Scheduler** -- `SUMOAPP__ADDITIONALINFO__C`, `SUMOAPP__ORGANIZERFULLNAME__C`, `SUMOAPP__SUMOID__C` (new scheduling integration)
- **Compliance** -- `TR_WC1__RESOLUTION_STATUS__C`, `TR_WC1__RISK_LEVEL__C`, `TR_WC1__SCREENINGCASEIDS__C` (new screening/compliance)

### Questions for Task/Event

| # | Question | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Is `ACTIVITY_EXTENSION__C` a lookup to a custom object?** If so, does `Activity_Extension__c` also need to be synced from GTM Next UAT? | Missing related object = broken lookups in `wd_task` / `wd_event` |
| 2 | **Gong integration is dropped** -- is this intentional or will Gong reconnect to GTM Next later? | Impacts activity attribution and call analytics downstream |
| 3 | **HVA (High Value Activity) Task fields dropped** -- is HVA being replaced by a different scoring system in GTM Next? | HVA_POINTS__C and HVA__C are still *common*, but the Task-[REDACTED] HVA fields are gone |
| 4 | **New `TR_WC1__*` compliance fields** -- should these be included in `wd_task` / `wd_event` or filtered out for compliance reasons? | Screening data may have restricted access requirements |
| 5 | **`SUMOAPP__*` fields** -- is SumoLogic Scheduler replacing any existing scheduling tool? Does it affect `ISRECURRENCE` logic? | Event recurrence pattern might differ between GTM Now and GTM Next |
| 6 | **Do Task/Event need SCD2 history tracking?** Current plan shows no SCD2 for these objects. | Activity status changes (open -> closed) may need point-in-time analysis for pipeline health |
| 7 | **`WHOID` / `WHATID` polymorphic lookups** -- will these resolve to GTM Next Contact/Lead/Account/Opportunity IDs only, or cross-reference GTM Now IDs too? | Critical for activity-to-opportunity and activity-to-contact joins |

---

## Lead & Contact

### Field Analysis Summary

| Metric | Lead | Contact |
|---|---|---|
| **Common fields** | ~498 | ~521 |
| **Dropped fields** | ~109 | ~128 |
| **New fields** | ~20 | ~90 |

### Key Dropped Integrations (Lead & Contact)
- **Pardot** -- all `PI__*` fields dropped (~20 fields each)
- **Bizible** -- all `BIZIBLE2__*` fields dropped (~12 fields each)
- **Gong** -- `GONG__*` flow/engagement fields dropped
- **Formula/rollup** -- `ACCOUNT_*__C` denormalized fields dropped (e.g., `ACCOUNT_HCM_STATUS__C`)

### Key New Fields - Contact (90+ new fields!)
- **`GTM_NEXT_*` migration fields** -- `GTM_NEXT_CDC_SYNC_READY__C`, `GTM_NEXT_CONTACT_DATASYNC__C`, `GTM_NEXT_CONTACT_ID__C`, `GTM_NEXT_RELATED_OBJECT_ID__C` (explicit migration tracking)
- **`CLC_*` lifecycle fields** (~40+ fields) -- `CLC_ENGAGEMENTSTATUS__C`, `CLC_MQLREASON__C`, `CLC_MQLTIER__C`, `CLC_FUNCTIONALROLE__C`, `CLC_ROUTINGREGION__C`, etc. (appears to be a new **Contact Lifecycle** framework in GTM Next)
- **`CMN_*` common fields** -- `CMN_LEGACYSALESFORCEID__C`, `CMN_LEGACYSOURCE__C`, `CMN_LEGACYUSERID__C`, `CMN_INT_EXTERNALID__C` (cross-org migration/mapping fields)

### Questions for Lead & Contact

| # | Question | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Contact has `GTM_NEXT_CONTACT_ID__C` and `GTM_NEXT_CDC_SYNC_READY__C`** -- is this a bidirectional sync flag between GTM Now and GTM Next? | Determines if Contact dedup logic is needed or if Salesforce handles it |
| 2 | **New `CLC_*` prefix (~40 fields)** -- is this a renamed/migrated version of existing engagement fields, or a new Contact Lifecycle Cloud module? | Maps directly to whether `wd_contact` should carry both old and new field names |
| 3 | **`CMN_LEGACYSALESFORCEID__C`** on Contact -- does this store the GTM Now Contact ID? | This is the cross-reference key for coexistence; critical for Sigma BI stitching |
| 4 | **Lead has no `GTM_NEXT_*` migration fields** -- is Lead migration happening differently than Contact? | If Leads are not migrated (only new leads in GTM Next), the coexistence story is simpler |
| 5 | **Pardot (`PI__*`) dropped from both** -- what replaces Pardot for marketing scoring/attribution in GTM Next? | Affects `LEADSOURCE` attribution logic and marketing-sourced pipeline reporting |
| 6 | **`DASCOOPCOMPOSER__*` (Groove/Clari) expanding** on both Lead & Contact -- is Groove the primary sales engagement platform for GTM Next? | Groove fields drive activity rollup and sequence attribution |
| 7 | **Lead has ~498 common fields** -- will GTM Next Lead have a reduced footprint, or carry all legacy fields? | Affects `wd_lead` column count and whether a "slim" lead model is appropriate |
| 8 | **Contact-to-Account relationship** -- is `ACCOUNTID` on Contact guaranteed to reference a GTM Next Account, or could it point to a GTM Now Account? | Determines join strategy for `wd_contact` -> `wd_account` |

---

## UAT Schema-Specific Questions

| # | Question | Why It Matters |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Will the schema name change** from `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT` to `SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT` when prod is live? | dbt source definitions should use a **variable** (`var('gtmnext_schema', 'SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT')`) so the switch is a config change, not a code change |
| 2 | **Is UAT data representative** -- same record types, custom fields, and integrations as prod will have? | Field-level differences between UAT and Prod would break models at cutover |
| 3 | **UAT data volume** -- is it sample/sandbox data or a full copy? | Affects incremental model testing and performance validation |
| 4 | **Fivetran sync frequency** for UAT -- same cadence as production will be? | Determines if freshness tests should be relaxed for UAT |
| 5 | **Will `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT_UAT` persist after prod cutover**, or will it be dropped? | If it persists, need a decommission plan; if dropped, need source switch automation |
| 6 | **Should dbt models point to `BASE_PROD` directly**, or should there be a `RAW_PROD` landing first (as in the current architecture doc)? | Current docs show `RAW_PROD.SALESFORCE_GTMNEXT` -> `BASE_PROD_NEXT`. The UAT path skips RAW and goes straight to BASE_PROD |

## 369. assistant

Yes, **Task is connected to Opportunity** — but through a **polymorphic lookup**, not a direct foreign key. Here's how it works:

### Task → Opportunity Relationship

| Field | Type | What It Does |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `WHATID` | Polymorphic lookup (common) | References the "related to" record — can point to **Opportunity**, Account, Case, or any other object. This is the primary link. |
| `ACCOUNTID` | Lookup (common) | Direct link to Account (auto-populated by Salesforce based on WHATID resolution) |
| `WHOID` | Polymorphic lookup (common) | References the "name" (person) — points to Contact or Lead |
| `OPPORTUNITY_STAGE_AT_TIME_OF_ACTIVITY__C` | Custom field (common) | Captures the Opportunity stage at the time the Task was created — confirms Tasks are actively used in the Opportunity context |

### Key Points

1. **`WHATID` is the join key** — When a Task is logged against an Opportunity, `WHATID` contains the Opportunity ID. But it can also hold an Account ID, Case ID, etc. You need to resolve the polymorphic type (typically via `WHATID` prefix `006` = Opportunity, `001` = Account).

2. **`OPPORTUNITY_STAGE_AT_TIME_OF_ACTIVITY__C`** is common in both GTM Now and GTM Next — this is a denormalized snapshot, confirming that Task-[REDACTED] activity tracking continues.

3. **`OPPORTUNITY_OWNER_AT_CREATION__C`** is **dropped** in GTM Next — this denormalized owner field is being removed. If downstream models relied on this, they'll need to join `wd_task` → `wd_opportunity` → owner instead.

### For dbt Models

The typical join pattern would be:

```sql
-- Task to Opportunity join (resolving polymorphic WHATID)
SELECT t.*, o.stagename, o.amount
FROM wd_task t
LEFT JOIN wd_opportunity o
  ON t.whatid = o.id
WHERE LEFT(t.whatid, 3) = '006'  -- Salesforce Opportunity key prefix
```

Or if using `TaskRelation` / `EventRelation` (shared activities), the grain changes to many-to-many — which is why question **4.7** in the document asks whether GTM Next `WHATID` resolves to GTM Next Opportunity IDs only, or could cross-reference GTM Now IDs too.

## 378. assistant

Excellent. Now the full picture is clear. Here's the complete analysis:

---

## TaskRelation & EventRelation in GTM Now

Both tables **already exist** in `BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE` and are actively populated. They enable Salesforce's **"Shared Activities"** feature — allowing a single Task or Event to be related to **multiple** records simultaneously.

### What They Do

**Without** TaskRelation/EventRelation (legacy model):
- Task.`WHATID` → one related record (Opportunity, Account, etc.)
- Task.`WHOID` → one person (Contact or Lead)
- **1:1 relationship only**

**With** TaskRelation/EventRelation (shared activities):
- A single Task/Event can be linked to **multiple Contacts**, **multiple Opportunities**, etc.
- The relation junction table holds **each relationship as a separate row**

### Schema

**TaskRelation:**

| Column | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `TASKID` | FK to `TASK.ID` |
| `RELATIONID` | Polymorphic FK — the related record (Contact, Opportunity, Account, Agreement, etc.) |
| `ISWHAT` | `TRUE` = "Related To" (Opportunity, Account, etc.), `FALSE` = "Name" (Contact, Lead) |
| `ACCOUNTID` | Auto-resolved Account |

**EventRelation** has the same plus:

| Column | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `ISINVITEE` | Whether this person was invited to the event |
| `ISPARENT` | Whether this is the parent/organizer |
| `STATUS` | Invitee response status |
| `RESPONSE` / `RESPONDEDDATE` | Invitee response details |

### Live Data in GTM Now

**TaskRelation: 62.3M rows** across 52.1M distinct Tasks:

| Related Object | ISWHAT | Count | What It Means |
|---------------|--------|-------|---------------|
| **Contact** | FALSE (Who) | 40.6M | Task linked to a Contact |
| **Product Configuration** | TRUE (What) | 4.6M | Task linked to CPQ config |
| **Opportunity** | TRUE (What) | **4.5M** | Task linked to an Opportunity |
| **Agreement** | TRUE (What) | 4.0M | Task linked to an Agreement |
| **Lead** | FALSE (Who) | 3.1M | Task linked to a Lead |
| **Account** | TRUE (What) | 2.6M | Task linked to an Account |
| **Quote/Proposal** | TRUE (What) | 1.4M | Task linked to a Proposal |
| **Customer Tenant** | TRUE (What) | 1.2M | Task linked to a Tenant |

**EventRelation: 5.6M rows** across 3.9M distinct Events:

| Related Object | ISWHAT | Count |
|---------------|--------|-------|
| **Opportunity** | TRUE (What) | **2.1M** |
| **Contact** | FALSE (Who) | 1.6M |
| **Account** | TRUE (What) | 1.2M |
| **User** | FALSE (Who) | 537K (internal attendees) |

### Multi-Relation Distribution (Grain Impact)

**Tasks with multiple relations:**

| Relations per Task | Task Count |
|-------------------|-----------|
| 1 relation | 44.1M (85%) |
| 2 relations | 7.0M (13%) |
| 3 relations | 536K |
| 4+ relations | ~500K |

**Key finding from Task table itself:**
- **All Tasks have single `WHATID`** (no multi-what on Task)
- **1.27M Tasks have multiple WHOs** (multi-contact emails/calls)

This means: on Tasks, `WHATID` gives you one Opportunity, but TaskRelation gives you the **additional** linked Contacts/Leads.

### Why This Matters for GTM Next

1. **Task → Opportunity is confirmed**: 4.5M TaskRelation rows link Tasks to Opportunities, and 2.1M EventRelation rows link Events to Opportunities. The connection is heavy.

2. **TaskRelation/EventRelation is the authoritative many-to-many join table** — not `WHATID`/`WHOID` alone. If you only use `WHATID`, you get the *primary* related record. TaskRelation shows *all* related records.

3. **Critical for GTM Next**: We need to confirm that `TASKRELATION` and `EVENTRELATION` are also being synced from the GTM Next UAT instance. Without them, multi-contact activity attribution breaks.

4. **Agreement/Proposal links**: 4.0M Task-[REDACTED] and 1.4M Task-[REDACTED] relations — these are used for contract lifecycle tracking and should flow into `wd_task` models.

Shall I add a question about TaskRelation/EventRelation Fivetran sync to the R1 Objects Questions document?

---

### Key Takeaways

**15 P0 (Blocker) questions** identified that must be answered before any GTM Next finance work can begin:

1. **Agreement Line Item is gutted** — 30+ fields used directly by `stg_em_int_agree_base.sql` are DROPPED, including `TOTAL_FEES__C`, `TCV__C`, `ACV__C`, `PRODUCT_SKU__C`, term dates, employee counts, and worker breakdowns. ## YES — Required for Finance Pipeline (17 tables)

These tables from the GTM Next list have **direct equivalents currently used** in the finance data flow:

| # | GTM Next Table | Current Pipeline Use | Maps to SCD2 Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | `APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__C` | Agreement header — core of ARR/FLA | `UNIFIED_HISTORY_AGREEMENT_SCD2` |
| **2** | `APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__HISTORY` | Field-level history for SCD2 | Feeds SCD2 build |
| **3** | `APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__C` | **#1 table for ARR** — TOTAL_FEES, term dates, SKU | `UNIFIED_HISTORY_AGREEMENT_LINE_ITEM_SCD2` |
| **4** | `APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__HISTORY` | Field-level history for SCD2 | Feeds SCD2 build |
| **5** | `APTTUS__APTS_RELATED_AGREEMENT__C` | **SSR logic** — supersede & replace | `UNIFIED_HISTORY_RELATED_AGREEMENT_SCD2` |
| **6** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__LINEITEM__C` | Config line items — agreement line source | `UNIFIED_HISTORY_CONFIG_LINE_ITEM_SCD2` |
| **7** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__ORDERLINEITEM__C` | **NEW** — has `IMPACTARR__C`, `IMPACTTCV__C`, `ORDERARR__C` | New SCD2 needed |
| **8** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__ORDER__C` | Order header — links to Agreement | New SCD2 needed |
| **9** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__ASSETLINEITEM__C` | Asset tracking — renewal baseline | Referenced in GTM Now |
| **10** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRICELISTITEM__C` | Price list items — pricing reference | Product pricing |
| **11** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRICELIST__C` | Price lists | Pricing context |
| **12** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRODUCTCLASSIFICATION__C` | Product classification hierarchy | Product/SKU mapping |
| **13** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRODUCTGROUPMEMBER__C` | Product group membership | Product hierarchy |
| **14** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRODUCTGROUP__C` | Product grouping | Product hierarchy |
| **15** | `APTTUS_CONFIG2__PRODUCTOPTIONCOMPONENT__C` | Bundle/option structure | SKU bundle logic |
| **16** | `APTTUS_APPROVAL__APPROVAL_REQUEST__C` | Approval status on agreements | Used in `bt_sku_analytics` filter |
| **17** | `APTTUS__AGREEMENTHIERARCHY__C` | Agreement parent-child | SSR/amendment flows |

**Created: [EDAEM-3825](https://workdaybt.atlassian.net/browse/EDAEM-3825) — GTM Next - Continuous Monitoring & ARR Validation for Synced Agreements**

**What it covers:**

| Monitoring Area | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Daily Volume Tracking** | Count of agreements/ALIs with `GTM_NEXT_*_ID` populated |
| **SCD2 Propagation** | Verify records appear in `UNIFIED_HISTORY_*_SCD2` within 24 hours |
| **FLA Processing** | Confirm all activated, in-term synced agreements flow into Finance Line Analytics |
| **ARR Correctness** | Validate `ARR = TCV * (365 / term_days)` and correct category assignment |
| **No Duplicate ARR** | Same deal not counted twice (GTM Now + GTM Next paths) |
| **Currency Conversion** | CAD/EUR agreements correctly converted via DatedConversionRate |
| **SSR Logic** | GTM Next superseding agreements get correct SSR category |

**Alerting Criteria:**
- Agreement missing from FLA after 48 hours
- ARR variance > $1 vs expected
- Duplicate agreement IDs in FLA
- Failed SCD2 processing

**Linked to:** EDAEM-3777 (relates) | EDAEM-3824 (blocked by Prod Deploy)

**Full story chain:**
`EDAEM-3777` (Initial Validation) → `EDAEM-3821` (Build) → `EDAEM-3822` (QA) → `EDAEM-3823` (UAT) → `EDAEM-3824` (Prod Deploy) → **`EDAEM-3825`** (Continuous Monitoring)

```sql
       APTTUS__CONTRACT_START_DATE__C, APTTUS__CONTRACT_END_DATE__C,
       LASTMODIFIEDDATE, _FIVETRAN_SYNCED
FROM BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE.APTTUS__APTS_AGREEMENT__C
WHERE GTM_NEXT_AGREEMENT_ID__C IS NOT NULL
   OR GTM_NEXT_ORDER_ID__C IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY _FIVETRAN_SYNCED DESC
```

**ALI table — using `GTM_NEXT_ORDERPRODUCT_ID__C`:**

```sql
SELECT ID, NAME, APTTUS__AGREEMENTID__C,
       GTM_NEXT_ORDERPRODUCT_ID__C,
       APTTUS_CMCONFIG__LINESTATUS__C, APTTUS__QUANTITY__C,
       TOTAL_FEES__C, ACV__C, PEPY__C, CURRENCYISOCODE,
       APTS_TERM_START_DATE__C, APTS_TERM_END_DATE__C,
       LASTMODIFIEDDATE, _FIVETRAN_SYNCED
FROM BASE_PROD.SALESFORCE.APTTUS__AGREEMENTLINEITEM__C
WHERE GTM_NEXT_ORDERPRODUCT_ID__C IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY _FIVETRAN_SYNCED DESC
```

## 4 ALIs Dropped at `DAILY_SNAPSHOT → FLA` (the FLA TVF filtering logic)

| ALI Dropped | Fees | GTM Next ID | Probable Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| `a1aVT00000SsT05YAF` | $0 CAD | None | No GTM Next ID — likely original pre-sync placeholder line, filtered by product join or dedup |

## Key Takeaway

The **drop point is `DAILY_SNAPSHOT → FLA`** — specifically inside the **FLA TVF (Table-Valued Function)** which applies:
1. Product hierarchy join (if SKU/product not found in product mapping → excluded)
2. Deduplication logic (ROW_NUMBER partitioned by agreement line PK)
3. Agreement type/status filters

The pipeline layers `SCD2 → DAILY_SNAPSHOT` pass everything through (11/11). The FLA TVF filtering logic then reduces to 7/11, and from FLA onward nothing else drops (7/7 pass to ARR Line Categories).

## 650. assistant

Set up Miro through the REST API instead, using the same token-based pattern as your Jira access. The MCP entry rolled back when you cancelled the OAuth, so `mcp.json` is back to its original four servers (snowflake, dbt, salesforce, sigma).

## What I built

**A Miro API client** at `~/.claude/skills/miro-board-developer/miro_api.py` — standard library only, so it runs anywhere without a virtualenv. It reads boards and items, creates boards, and bulk-builds frames, shapes, sticky notes, and connectors from a JSON spec. It retries `429` and `5xx` with backoff, and gives a specific message for 401 vs 403 so a bad token is distinguishable from a missing scope.

**A rule** at `/Users/koteswararao.venkata/Documents/Cursor/.cursor/rules/miro-api-access.mdc` that makes agents reach for this path automatically on any Miro request. It explicitly overrides `mcp-restart-first` for Miro, so nothing will loop trying to re-auth the MCP server again.

## The one thing you need to do

Get a token from Miro's developer settings — this is the direct-install variety, so there's no redirect URI and no authorization-code exchange:

1. Go to https://miro.com/app/settings/user-profile/apps
2. **Create new app**, name it, pick your Developer team
3. Under **Permissions**, enable `boards:read` and `boards:write`
4. Under **Install app and get OAuth token**, click the button, pick the team holding your boards, and copy the token

Then add it to `~/.zshrc`:

```bash
export MIRO_ACCESS_TOKEN="<paste token here>"
```

Verify with:

```bash
source ~/.zshrc && python3 ~/.claude/skills/miro-board-developer/miro_api.py whoami
```

## Then it's usable right away

```bash
python3 ~/.claude/skills/miro-board-developer/miro_api.py boards
python3 ~/.claude/skills/miro-board-developer/miro_api.py items uXjVGqWc4qE=
python3 ~/.claude/skills/miro-board-developer/miro_api.py build uXjVGqWc4qE= flow.json
```

The `build` command is the one worth knowing about — it takes a JSON spec where each node has a local `key`, so connectors can reference nodes by name before their real Miro IDs exist. That means I can generate a whole data flow diagram in one pass rather than creating items and then wiring them up.

One caveat worth flagging: since Workday is on Miro Enterprise, if the Developer team app creation is restricted at the org level you'd need admin help. If step 2 doesn't let you create the app, tell me and I'll write up what to request. Once you have the token in place, send me a board URL and I'll build the GTM Next data flow onto it.

Now I need to guide the user through getting the token. The MCP entry rolled back when they cancelled, so it won't be in their mcp.json file. I'll keep this straightforward with the one action they need to take, and mention that while the full OAuth flow is available, the direct-install token is the simpler path forward.
